Like the competent person who just got promoted isn't really there anymore. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining that right.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this feeling that work isn't a safe place for you anymore?\n\nPatient: Well, it's... I mean, I know it's probably silly of me to even bring it up. I've just been trying to manage things a little differently lately, you know, just to be careful. I don't want to be a burden on my team or anything, but I've started, um, trading some of my shifts with coworkers, and maybe pushing back on our release windows just a little bit. \n\nAnd I've been trying to work from home whenever I can... or at least taking the side entrance when I do have to go into the office. It's just... I'm sorry, it's a little embarrassing to admit, but if I have to walk through that main lobby after dark, I just get this awful feeling. Like I’m going to completely come apart. \n\nI know that sounds like a huge overreaction, and I'm really trying not to let it affect my performance. Is that... is it okay that I'm doing that for now? I hope I don't sound completely unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding any other places or situations besides the main lobby at work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I've been trying to just stick to my normal routine as much as possible. I don't want this to turn into a whole big thing where I'm, you know, hiding from the world or anything. So it's really just that one specific spot at work. Everything else is... well, it's fine. I'm still doing everything I normally do. I hope that's a good sign? Like maybe it's not as bad as I'm making it out to be?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden feeling of intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I definitely get stressed out, especially lately, but... not like a sudden, intense panic or anything like that. I don't think I've ever had a full-blown panic attack, if that's what you mean. I usually just try to take a deep breath and keep going. I'm pretty good at holding it together in the moment, I think. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right.\n\nDoctor: Has something specific happened to you at work that made you start feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yes. I guess I should have just said that from the beginning. I'm sorry, I just... I don't want to sound like I'm still dwelling on it, you know? Because it's been a while, and I feel like I should be completely over it by now. \n\nBut, um, there was a break-in at the office. It was during an overnight deployment, so I was there late. And... well, that's when it happened. But that was, gosh, about two years and four months ago now. So it's really silly that I'm still getting so worked up about the lobby, isn't it? I mean, I know the building is secure now, and management handled everything really well. I just... I hope you don't think I'm being weak for still letting it bother me after all this time. Is it normal to still feel a little jumpy after that long?\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding talking or thinking about the break-in at the office?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. I mean, I try not to bring it up all the time because I don't want to be a bother or make anyone else uncomfortable. But if someone asks, I'll talk about it. And I definitely still think about it sometimes, but I really try to just push those thoughts away and focus on my work. I don't want it to seem like I'm obsessed with it or anything. I'm trying really hard to move past it, I promise. Is that the right way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing to get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're not as capable or competent as you used to be?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I have been struggling with that a little bit. I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party or anything, but... yeah. I just can't seem to shake this feeling that work isn't a safe place anymore. And... I don't know, it's almost like I feel a little bit damaged now? \n\nLike, I used to be so on top of things, and I worked really hard to get promoted, but lately I just feel like... like that competent person isn't really there anymore. I mean, I still get my work done, I really do! I'm trying my absolute best. But I just don't feel as sharp as I used to be. Is that... is that a normal thing to feel? I really hope I'm not just making excuses for myself. I want to be the employee I used to be, I really do.\n\nDoctor: Do you have memories of the break-in that still feel really vivid or disturbing to you?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to let them get to me. I know I should be focusing on the present, and I don't want to sound dramatic. But, um... yes, I guess I do have some memories that pop up like that. I hope that doesn't mean I'm failing at moving on. \n\nSometimes, if I hear a badge scanner beeping, or... or just heavy footsteps out in the hallway... or even if I get a late-night deployment alert... it's like I'm suddenly put right back in that office. My chest gets all tight, and... um... I'm sorry, I don't want to be too graphic or make you uncomfortable, but... I just get this sudden image of the guard on the floor. It just cuts right through whatever I’m doing. I really do try to shake it off right away and get back to work, I promise. Is it okay that I still get those flashes? I'm really trying my best to just push through them so I'm not a burden on my team.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think that you're worthless or inadequate as a person?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I definitely have my moments where I feel like I'm not doing as well at my job as I used to, like I mentioned, but... I don't think I'm worthless as a person. I know I still have good qualities, and I try really hard to be a good friend and a good employee. \n\nI mean, I hope I don't come across as someone who just feels sorry for themselves all the time. Is it okay that I feel that way? Um... I really don't want to sound like a pessimist, because I'm usually a very positive person. I always try to look on the bright side of things. But... yeah, I guess I do feel that way sometimes.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, it's been a while, I guess. I mean, I really have been trying to force myself to just be normal about it, but... it's actually been going on since the break-in happened. Which was... um, during that overnight deployment about two years and four months ago.

I just hope you don't think less of me for not being able to just walk through the front door like everyone else.
